I guess what finally made me come in is... it’s this thing at work that I can’t get out of my head. I work with buys and launch numbers, and I keep feeling like people at work — and honestly even some of the vendors — are tracking what I’m buying or changing things around, like messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid or like I don’t know what I’m doing.

And I know how that sounds. My supervisor has shown me reports and said, basically, “Look, the numbers don’t prove that,” or that it’s just normal changes, but it still feels like there’s something going on behind it. Like I can see how it lines up too perfectly sometimes. I keep going over it and over it, and I can’t really shake it, and it’s starting to mess with me enough that I figured I should talk to someone.

Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word, like I’m not saying they’re trying to physically hurt me or anything. But deceive me? Make me look bad? Yeah, that’s what it feels like.

It’s like they’re watching what I’m buying, tracking the buys somehow, and then the launch numbers come out weird or get changed in a way that makes it look like I made a bad call. And I know my supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me that the numbers don’t prove anyone is doing that, but I still can’t shake it. I keep thinking, okay, maybe the report doesn’t show it because they know how to make it look normal.

That’s really the thing that made me come in, because I keep going over it in my head and it feels too specific to just be random.

It’s mostly work. Like, I don’t really sit at home thinking my friends are doing that kind of thing to me, if that’s what you mean. It’s very tied to the buys, the vendors, the launch numbers — that whole system.

That’s why it’s been hard for me to just dismiss it, because it feels connected to actual things I’m responsible for. I’ll make a buying decision, or I’ll be watching how something is supposed to launch, and then the numbers come out in a way that feels off, like somebody knew what I was doing and adjusted things so I’d look wrong. And even when my supervisor pulls up the reports and says, “See, this doesn’t show anyone changed anything,” I still keep thinking maybe the report just doesn’t show the part that matters, or maybe they’re covering it in a way that looks normal.

So yeah, I’d say it’s mainly in the work environment. It’s not like every single person in my life feels suspicious to me. It’s more that at work, with the vendors and the tracking of buys, I can’t get rid of the feeling that something is being done on purpose to make me look stupid.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really outside of work, no. I mean, I can get in my head about it after I leave work, like I’ll be at home thinking through the launch numbers or replaying what a vendor said, but the actual feeling is still about work.

It’s not like I think my friends or random people are all secretly coordinating against me. It’s more specific than that. It’s the buys, the vendors, the reports, the launch numbers — that whole setup. That’s where it feels like people could be tracking what I’m buying or moving things around so it reflects badly on me.

And that’s kind of why it bothers me so much, because if it was just some random thought everywhere, maybe I could dismiss it easier. But it feels tied to real stuff I’m responsible for. Even when my supervisor shows me reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I keep thinking, yeah, but what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they’re making it look normal on purpose?

So no, I wouldn’t say it really happens outside of work in the same way. It’s mainly there.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I do question it sometimes, yeah. Like… that’s part of why I’m here. If I was completely sure, maybe I wouldn’t even be asking anyone about it. But it’s not like I can just talk myself out of it either.

My supervisor will show me the reports and say, basically, “Look, this doesn’t show anyone changed the launch numbers,” or “There’s no evidence the vendors are tracking your buys,” and in that moment I’ll try to listen. I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m reading too much into it. Maybe it’s just the numbers being weird, or normal changes, or whatever.

But then I leave and I keep going over it, and it starts feeling too specific again. Like the timing will line up with something I bought, or a launch will come out looking bad in a way that makes it seem like I made a stupid decision, and I can’t shake the feeling that someone is doing it on purpose. Not necessarily trying to hurt me physically or anything, but trying to make me look incompetent.

So I guess the honest answer is I’m not one hundred percent certain every second, but the feeling is really strong. Even when someone shows me proof that it doesn’t add up, I still think, well, what if the report just doesn’t show the part that matters? What if they know how to make it look normal? That’s what keeps getting stuck in my head.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… mostly I’ve kept bringing it up at work, especially with my supervisor. I’ll ask to look at the reports again, or I’ll point out where the launch numbers don’t make sense to me, because in my head it feels like there’s something there that should show it.

I haven’t been able to just let it go. Like, if a buy I made looks bad after the launch, I start going back through it and thinking, okay, who knew about this, who had access, did the vendor know what I was buying, did somebody adjust something after the fact? And then I’ll want the reports pulled up because I feel like there has to be some proof somewhere.

But the hard part is, even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I still don’t feel settled. I’ll sit there and think, “Okay, maybe,” but then another part of me is like, what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they know how to make it look normal?

So I guess yes, I’ve acted on it in the sense that I’ve questioned it, checked things, and brought it to my supervisor. And honestly, what really made me come in is that I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when I’m shown that the reports don’t really prove that, it still keeps coming back.

I’ll make a buying decision, or I’ll be watching how something is supposed to launch, and then the numbers come out in a way that feels off, like somebody knew what I was doing and adjusted things so I’d look wrong. And even when my supervisor pulls up the reports and says, “See, this doesn’t show anyone changed anything,” I still keep thinking maybe the report just doesn’t show the part that matters, or maybe they’re covering it in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo yeah, I’d say it’s mainly in the work environment. Even when someone shows me proof that it doesn’t add up, I still think, well, what if the report just doesn’t show the part that matters? What if they know how to make it look normal? That’s what keeps getting stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any actions based on these suspicions — like confronting someone, gathering evidence, or reporting it to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly I’ve kept bringing it up at work, especially with my supervisor. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really anything separate from that, no. I mean, I don’t have some whole other thing where I think strangers are doing something, or my friends are in on something, or anything like that. It mostly comes back to work, over and over.

The thing I can’t shake is the buys and the launch numbers. Like, I’ll make a buy, and then if the launch numbers come back in a way that makes the decision look dumb or like I missed something obvious, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like someone knew what I was doing, or was watching what I bought, and then something got moved around or adjusted so it would make me look bad.

And I know that probably sounds like I’m stuck on it, because I am. My supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me, like, “This doesn’t prove anyone changed anything,” or “There’s nothing here showing vendors are tracking your buys.” And I can sit there and understand what they’re saying, but I still leave with this feeling like the report just isn’t showing the part that matters. Like maybe they know how to make it look normal.

So if you mean other unusual beliefs outside of work, I don’t think so. But this one feels really real to me, even though other people don’t seem to see it the same way. That’s honestly the main reason I came in, because I keep trying to drop it and I just can’t.

Yeah, that’s what it feels like.\n\nIt’s like they’re watching what I’m buying, tracking the buys somehow, and then the launch numbers come out weird or get changed in a way that makes it look like I made a bad call. And I know my supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me that the numbers don’t prove anyone is doing that, but I still can’t shake it. I keep thinking, okay, maybe the report doesn’t show it because they know how to make it look normal.\n\nThat’s really the thing that made me come in, because I keep going over it in my head and it feels too specific to just be random.\n\nDoctor: Do these kinds of thoughts or feelings happen anywhere else besides work, like at home or with friends, or is this something that only seems to happen in your work environment?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly work. Like, I don’t really sit at home thinking my friends are doing that kind of thing to me, if that’s what you mean. It’s very tied to the buys, the vendors, the launch numbers — that whole system.\n\nThat’s why it’s been hard for me to just dismiss it, because it feels connected to actual things I’m responsible for. I’ll make a buying decision, or I’ll be watching how something is supposed to launch, and then the numbers come out in a way that feels off, like somebody knew what I was doing and adjusted things so I’d look wrong. And even when my supervisor pulls up the reports and says, “See, this doesn’t show anyone changed anything,” I still keep thinking maybe the report just doesn’t show the part that matters, or maybe they’re covering it in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo yeah, I’d say it’s mainly in the work environment. It’s not like every single person in my life feels suspicious to me. It’s more that at work, with the vendors and the tracking of buys, I can’t get rid of the feeling that something is being done on purpose to make me look stupid.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences outside of work where you felt people were acting together in secret or manipulating things in a way that was specifically targeted at you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. I mean, I can get in my head about it after I leave work, like I’ll be at home thinking through the launch numbers or replaying what a vendor said, but the actual feeling is still about work.\n\nIt’s not like I think my friends or random people are all secretly coordinating against me. It’s more specific than that. It’s the buys, the vendors, the reports, the launch numbers — that whole setup. That’s where it feels like people could be tracking what I’m buying or moving things around so it reflects badly on me.\n\nAnd that’s kind of why it bothers me so much, because if it was just some random thought everywhere, maybe I could dismiss it easier. But it feels tied to real stuff I’m responsible for. Even when my supervisor shows me reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I keep thinking, yeah, but what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they’re making it look normal on purpose?\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it really happens outside of work in the same way. It’s mainly there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever question whether your concerns about the buys and launch numbers might not be accurate, or do you feel certain that something is being done on purpose to make you look bad?\n\nPatient: I do question it sometimes, yeah. Like… that’s part of why I’m here. If I was completely sure, maybe I wouldn’t even be asking anyone about it. But it’s not like I can just talk myself out of it either.\n\nMy supervisor will show me the reports and say, basically, “Look, this doesn’t show anyone changed the launch numbers,” or “There’s no evidence the vendors are tracking your buys,” and in that moment I’ll try to listen. I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m reading too much into it. Maybe it’s just the numbers being weird, or normal changes, or whatever.\n\nBut then I leave and I keep going over it, and it starts feeling too specific again. Like the timing will line up with something I bought, or a launch will come out looking bad in a way that makes it seem like I made a stupid decision, and I can’t shake the feeling that someone is doing it on purpose. Not necessarily trying to hurt me physically or anything, but trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I’m not one hundred percent certain every second, but the feeling is really strong. Even when someone shows me proof that it doesn’t add up, I still think, well, what if the report just doesn’t show the part that matters? What if they know how to make it look normal? That’s what keeps getting stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any actions based on these suspicions — like confronting someone, gathering evidence, or reporting it to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly I’ve kept bringing it up at work, especially with my supervisor. I’ll ask to look at the reports again, or I’ll point out where the launch numbers don’t make sense to me, because in my head it feels like there’s something there that should show it.\n\nI haven’t been able to just let it go. Like, if a buy I made looks bad after the launch, I start going back through it and thinking, okay, who knew about this, who had access, did the vendor know what I was buying, did somebody adjust something after the fact? And then I’ll want the reports pulled up because I feel like there has to be some proof somewhere.\n\nBut the hard part is, even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I still don’t feel settled. I’ll sit there and think, “Okay, maybe,” but then another part of me is like, what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they know how to make it look normal?\n\nSo I guess yes, I’ve acted on it in the sense that I’ve questioned it, checked things, and brought it to my supervisor. And honestly, what really made me come in is that I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when I’m shown that the reports don’t really prove that, it still keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs or experiences — even outside of work — where you felt certain something was happening that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Not really anything separate from that, no. I mean, I don’t have some whole other thing where I think strangers are doing something, or my friends are in on something, or anything like that. It mostly comes back to work, over and over.\n\nThe thing I can’t shake is the buys and the launch numbers. Like, I’ll make a buy, and then if the launch numbers come back in a way that makes the decision look dumb or like I missed something obvious, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like someone knew what I was doing, or was watching what I bought, and then something got moved around or adjusted so it would make me look bad.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds like I’m stuck on it, because I am. My supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me, like, “This doesn’t prove anyone changed anything,” or “There’s nothing here showing vendors are tracking your buys.” And I can sit there and understand what they’re saying, but I still leave with this feeling like the report just isn’t showing the part that matters. Like maybe they know how to make it look normal.\n\nSo if you mean other unusual beliefs outside of work, I don’t think so. But this one feels really real to me, even though other people don’t seem to see it the same way. That’s honestly the main reason I came in, because I keep trying to drop it and I just can’t.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Like… that’s part of why I’m here. If I was completely sure, maybe I wouldn’t even be asking anyone about it. But it’s not like I can just talk myself out of it either.\n\nMy supervisor will show me the reports and say, basically, “Look, this doesn’t show anyone changed the launch numbers,” or “There’s no evidence the vendors are tracking your buys,” and in that moment I’ll try to listen. I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m reading too much into it. Maybe it’s just the numbers being weird, or normal changes, or whatever.\n\nBut then I leave and I keep going over it, and it starts feeling too specific again. Like the timing will line up with something I bought, or a launch will come out looking bad in a way that makes it seem like I made a stupid decision, and I can’t shake the feeling that someone is doing it on purpose. Not necessarily trying to hurt me physically or anything, but trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I’m not one hundred percent certain every second, but the feeling is really strong. Even when someone shows me proof that it doesn’t add up, I still think, well, what if the report just doesn’t show the part that matters? What if they know how to make it look normal? That’s what keeps getting stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any actions based on these suspicions — like confronting someone, gathering evidence, or reporting it to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly I’ve kept bringing it up at work, especially with my supervisor. I’ll ask to look at the reports again, or I’ll point out where the launch numbers don’t make sense to me, because in my head it feels like there’s something there that should show it.\n\nI haven’t been able to just let it go. Like, if a buy I made looks bad after the launch, I start going back through it and thinking, okay, who knew about this, who had access, did the vendor know what I was buying, did somebody adjust something after the fact? And then I’ll want the reports pulled up because I feel like there has to be some proof somewhere.\n\nBut the hard part is, even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I still don’t feel settled. I’ll sit there and think, “Okay, maybe,” but then another part of me is like, what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they know how to make it look normal?\n\nSo I guess yes, I’ve acted on it in the sense that I’ve questioned it, checked things, and brought it to my supervisor. And honestly, what really made me come in is that I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when I’m shown that the reports don’t really prove that, it still keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs or experiences — even outside of work — where you felt certain something was happening that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Not really anything separate from that, no. I mean, I don’t have some whole other thing where I think strangers are doing something, or my friends are in on something, or anything like that. It mostly comes back to work, over and over.\n\nThe thing I can’t shake is the buys and the launch numbers. Like, I’ll make a buy, and then if the launch numbers come back in a way that makes the decision look dumb or like I missed something obvious, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like someone knew what I was doing, or was watching what I bought, and then something got moved around or adjusted so it would make me look bad.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds like I’m stuck on it, because I am. My supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me, like, “This doesn’t prove anyone changed anything,” or “There’s nothing here showing vendors are tracking your buys.” And I can sit there and understand what they’re saying, but I still leave with this feeling like the report just isn’t showing the part that matters. Like maybe they know how to make it look normal.\n\nSo if you mean other unusual beliefs outside of work, I don’t think so. But this one feels really real to me, even though other people don’t seem to see it the same way. That’s honestly the main reason I came in, because I keep trying to drop it and I just can’t.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually upbeat or energized, maybe even more confident or irritable than usual, where you didn’t need much sleep or felt like you could take on a lot more than usual?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / wholesale_or_retail_buyer / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\nmanifestation: \"I get these stretches where I feel like the whole city is opening up for me and then, if someone questions a purchase order or tells me to slow down, I snap way harder than I mean to.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to know what counts as “unusual” because sometimes I’ll tell myself I’m just having a good stretch, like I’m finally getting ahead of things. But there are definitely times where I feel almost like everything is opening up for me, like the whole city is just… available, if that makes sense. Like I can move faster, make decisions faster, see opportunities other people aren’t seeing.

And when I’m in that kind of state, I’ll feel way more confident about what I’m doing at work. Like a purchase order will seem obvious to me, and I’ll feel like, okay, I’ve got this, I know where things are going, I can handle way more than people think I can. But then if someone questions it, or tells me to slow down, or acts like I need to explain myself more, I can get really irritated. Not just a little annoyed — I mean I’ll snap harder than I mean to. I’ll hear myself getting sharp, and part of me knows I’m coming off too intense, but in the moment it feels like they’re in my way or they’re not keeping up.

So… yeah, I do get those stretches where I’m more energized and confident, and sometimes it flips into being really irritable if people push back. The sleep part, I don’t know how to explain without overthinking it, but the main thing is that feeling like I can take on a lot more than usual, and then reacting badly when someone tries to slow me down.

It’s not like it’s just one afternoon and then I’m fine. That’s part of why it’s been hard to explain, because it kind of comes in stretches, and then I look back and realize I’ve been running hotter than normal for a while.

It feels like someone knew what I was doing, or was watching what I bought, and then something got moved around or adjusted so it would make me look bad.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds like I’m stuck on it, because I am. My supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me, like, “This doesn’t prove anyone changed anything,” or “There’s nothing here showing vendors are tracking your buys.” And I can sit there and understand what they’re saying, but I still leave with this feeling like the report just isn’t showing the part that matters. Like maybe they know how to make it look normal.\n\nSo if you mean other unusual beliefs outside of work, I don’t think so. But this one feels really real to me, even though other people don’t seem to see it the same way. That’s honestly the main reason I came in, because I keep trying to drop it and I just can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually upbeat or energized, maybe even more confident or irritable than usual, where you didn’t need much sleep or felt like you could take on a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to know what counts as “unusual” because sometimes I’ll tell myself I’m just having a good stretch, like I’m finally getting ahead of things. But there are definitely times where I feel almost like everything is opening up for me, like the whole city is just… available, if that makes sense. Like I can move faster, make decisions faster, see opportunities other people aren’t seeing.\n\nAnd when I’m in that kind of state, I’ll feel way more confident about what I’m doing at work. Like a purchase order will seem obvious to me, and I’ll feel like, okay, I’ve got this, I know where things are going, I can handle way more than people think I can. But then if someone questions it, or tells me to slow down, or acts like I need to explain myself more, I can get really irritated. Not just a little annoyed — I mean I’ll snap harder than I mean to. I’ll hear myself getting sharp, and part of me knows I’m coming off too intense, but in the moment it feels like they’re in my way or they’re not keeping up.\n\nSo… yeah, I do get those stretches where I’m more energized and confident, and sometimes it flips into being really irritable if people push back. The sleep part, I don’t know how to explain without overthinking it, but the main thing is that feeling like I can take on a lot more than usual, and then reacting badly when someone tries to slow me down.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat, energized, or confident usually last? Do they go away on their own, or do they last for days or even weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: It’s not like it’s just one afternoon and then I’m fine. That’s part of why it’s been hard to explain, because it kind of comes in stretches, and then I look back and realize I’ve been running hotter than normal for a while.\n\nI’d say these elevated, irritable, high-energy periods have been happening for about… maybe eleven months now, as part of my life. Not every single second of every day, but they’ve been coming up over that whole time. When I’m in it, it doesn’t feel brief. It can last long enough that people notice I’m acting different, and I don’t always come down from it just because I decide I should.\n\nSometimes it feels like it eases on its own eventually, but not in a clean way where I can say, “Oh, that was two hours and now it’s over.” It’s more like days where I’m more keyed up, more confident, more easily irritated, and then maybe it fades, and then later it comes back again. Like maybe they know how to make it look normal.\n\nSo if you mean other unusual beliefs outside of work, I don’t think so. But this one feels really real to me, even though other people don’t seem to see it the same way. That’s honestly the main reason I came in, because I keep trying to drop it and I just can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually upbeat or energized, maybe even more confident or irritable than usual, where you didn’t need much sleep or felt like you could take on a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to know what counts as “unusual” because sometimes I’ll tell myself I’m just having a good stretch, like I’m finally getting ahead of things. But there are definitely times where I feel almost like everything is opening up for me, like the whole city is just… available, if that makes sense. Like I can move faster, make decisions faster, see opportunities other people aren’t seeing.\n\nAnd when I’m in that kind of state, I’ll feel way more confident about what I’m doing at work. Like a purchase order will seem obvious to me, and I’ll feel like, okay, I’ve got this, I know where things are going, I can handle way more than people think I can. But then if someone questions it, or tells me to slow down, or acts like I need to explain myself more, I can get really irritated. Not just a little annoyed — I mean I’ll snap harder than I mean to. I’ll hear myself getting sharp, and part of me knows I’m coming off too intense, but in the moment it feels like they’re in my way or they’re not keeping up.\n\nSo… yeah, I do get those stretches where I’m more energized and confident, and sometimes it flips into being really irritable if people push back. The sleep part, I don’t know how to explain without overthinking it, but the main thing is that feeling like I can take on a lot more than usual, and then reacting badly when someone tries to slow me down.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat, energized, or confident usually last? Do they go away on their own, or do they last for days or even weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: It’s not like it’s just one afternoon and then I’m fine. That’s part of why it’s been hard to explain, because it kind of comes in stretches, and then I look back and realize I’ve been running hotter than normal for a while.\n\nI’d say these elevated, irritable, high-energy periods have been happening for about… maybe eleven months now, as part of my life. Not every single second of every day, but they’ve been coming up over that whole time. When I’m in it, it doesn’t feel brief. It can last long enough that people notice I’m acting different, and I don’t always come down from it just because I decide I should.\n\nSometimes it feels like it eases on its own eventually, but not in a clean way where I can say, “Oh, that was two hours and now it’s over.” It’s more like days where I’m more keyed up, more confident, more easily irritated, and then maybe it fades, and then later it comes back again. So overall, this has been going on for around eleven months.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period of time where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually care about, where it was hard to function normally?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / wholesale_or_retail_buyer / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Negative_Symptoms\nmanifestation: \"On my off days I mostly sit in my apartment scrolling through sneaker drops without really caring, and I dodge texts from friends about volleyball or gallery nights because it feels like too much work to answer.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly, but I do have these off days where I just kind of shut down. Like I’ll be in my apartment, and I’m not really doing anything, but somehow the whole day goes by. I’ll just sit there scrolling through sneaker drops, release calendars, resale pages, stuff like that — but not even in a way where I’m excited about it. It’s more like my thumb keeps moving and I’m staring at it, but I don’t really care if I buy anything or if something sells out or whatever.

And people will text me, like friends asking if I want to play volleyball or go to a gallery night or just meet up, and I’ll see the message come in and feel this immediate… heaviness, I guess. Like answering would mean I have to become a person again and explain myself or make a plan, and it just feels like too much work. So I dodge the texts. I’ll tell myself I’ll answer in ten minutes, and then it’s hours later, or the next day, and then I feel worse because now it seems awkward to respond.

It’s not always that I’m crying or lying in bed dramatically or anything. It’s more like everything takes too much effort, even stuff I normally like or people I actually care about. I just sit there and let the day disappear.

I work with buys and launch numbers, and I keep feeling like people at work — and honestly even some of the vendors — are tracking what I’m buying or changing things around, like messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid or like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nAnd I know how that sounds. My supervisor has shown me reports and said, basically, “Look, the numbers don’t prove that,” or that it’s just normal changes, but it still feels like there’s something going on behind it. I keep going over it and over it, and I can’t really shake it, and it’s starting to mess with me enough that I figured I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people or vendors at work are intentionally trying to harm or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word, like I’m not saying they’re trying to physically hurt me or anything. But deceive me? Make me look bad? Yeah, that’s what it feels like.\n\nIt’s like they’re watching what I’m buying, tracking the buys somehow, and then the launch numbers come out weird or get changed in a way that makes it look like I made a bad call. It’s not like every single person in my life feels suspicious to me. It’s more that at work, with the vendors and the tracking of buys, I can’t get rid of the feeling that something is being done on purpose to make me look stupid.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences outside of work where you felt people were acting together in secret or manipulating things in a way that was specifically targeted at you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. I mean, I can get in my head about it after I leave work, like I’ll be at home thinking through the launch numbers or replaying what a vendor said, but the actual feeling is still about work.\n\nIt’s not like I think my friends or random people are all secretly coordinating against me. I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m reading too much into it. Like the timing will line up with something I bought, or a launch will come out looking bad in a way that makes it seem like I made a stupid decision, and I can’t shake the feeling that someone is doing it on purpose. Not necessarily trying to hurt me physically or anything, but trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I’m not one hundred percent certain every second, but the feeling is really strong. Even when someone shows me proof that it doesn’t add up, I still think, well, what if the report just doesn’t show the part that matters? What if they know how to make it look normal? That’s what keeps getting stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any actions based on these suspicions — like confronting someone, gathering evidence, or reporting it to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly I’ve kept bringing it up at work, especially with my supervisor. Like, if a buy I made looks bad after the launch, I start going back through it and thinking, okay, who knew about this, who had access, did the vendor know what I was buying, did somebody adjust something after the fact? And then I’ll want the reports pulled up because I feel like there has to be some proof somewhere.\n\nBut the hard part is, even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I still don’t feel settled. I’ll sit there and think, “Okay, maybe,” but then another part of me is like, what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they know how to make it look normal?\n\nSo I guess yes, I’ve acted on it in the sense that I’ve questioned it, checked things, and brought it to my supervisor. And honestly, what really made me come in is that I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when I’m shown that the reports don’t really prove that, it still keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs or experiences — even outside of work — where you felt certain something was happening that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Not really anything separate from that, no. Like, I don’t really sit at home thinking my friends are doing that kind of thing to me, if that’s what you mean. I’ll make a buying decision, or I’ll be watching how something is supposed to launch, and then the numbers come out in a way that feels off, like somebody knew what I was doing and adjusted things so I’d look wrong. And even when my supervisor pulls up the reports and says, “See, this doesn’t show anyone changed anything,” I still keep thinking maybe the report just doesn’t show the part that matters, or maybe they’re covering it in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo yeah, I’d say it’s mainly in the work environment. But it’s not like I can just talk myself out of it either.\n\nMy supervisor will show me the reports and say, basically, “Look, this doesn’t show anyone changed the launch numbers,” or “There’s no evidence the vendors are tracking your buys,” and in that moment I’ll try to listen. I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m reading too much into it. Maybe it’s just the numbers being weird, or normal changes, or whatever.\n\nBut then I leave and I keep going over it, and it starts feeling too specific again. Like the timing will line up with something I bought, or a launch will come out looking bad in a way that makes it seem like I made a stupid decision, and I can’t shake the feeling that someone is doing it on purpose. Not necessarily trying to hurt me physically or anything, but trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I’m not one hundred percent certain every second, but the feeling is really strong. Even when someone shows me proof that it doesn’t add up, I still think, well, what if the report just doesn’t show the part that matters? What if they know how to make it look normal? That’s what keeps getting stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any actions based on these suspicions — like confronting someone, gathering evidence, or reporting it to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly I’ve kept bringing it up at work, especially with my supervisor. I’ll ask to look at the reports again, or I’ll point out where the launch numbers don’t make sense to me, because in my head it feels like there’s something there that should show it.\n\nI haven’t been able to just let it go. Like, if a buy I made looks bad after the launch, I start going back through it and thinking, okay, who knew about this, who had access, did the vendor know what I was buying, did somebody adjust something after the fact? And then I’ll want the reports pulled up because I feel like there has to be some proof somewhere.\n\nBut the hard part is, even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I still don’t feel settled. I’ll sit there and think, “Okay, maybe,” but then another part of me is like, what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they know how to make it look normal?\n\nSo I guess yes, I’ve acted on it in the sense that I’ve questioned it, checked things, and brought it to my supervisor. And honestly, what really made me come in is that I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when I’m shown that the reports don’t really prove that, it still keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs or experiences — even outside of work — where you felt certain something was happening that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Not really anything separate from that, no. I mean, I don’t have some whole other thing where I think strangers are doing something, or my friends are in on something, or anything like that. It mostly comes back to work, over and over.\n\nThe thing I can’t shake is the buys and the launch numbers. Like, I’ll make a buy, and then if the launch numbers come back in a way that makes the decision look dumb or like I missed something obvious, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like someone knew what I was doing, or was watching what I bought, and then something got moved around or adjusted so it would make me look bad.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds like I’m stuck on it, because I am. My supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me, like, “This doesn’t prove anyone changed anything,” or “There’s nothing here showing vendors are tracking your buys.” And I can sit there and understand what they’re saying, but I still leave with this feeling like the report just isn’t showing the part that matters. Like maybe they know how to make it look normal.\n\nSo if you mean other unusual beliefs outside of work, I don’t think so. But this one feels really real to me, even though other people don’t seem to see it the same way. That’s honestly the main reason I came in, because I keep trying to drop it and I just can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually upbeat or energized, maybe even more confident or irritable than usual, where you didn’t need much sleep or felt like you could take on a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to know what counts as “unusual” because sometimes I’ll tell myself I’m just having a good stretch, like I’m finally getting ahead of things. But there are definitely times where I feel almost like everything is opening up for me, like the whole city is just… available, if that makes sense. Like I can move faster, make decisions faster, see opportunities other people aren’t seeing.\n\nAnd when I’m in that kind of state, I’ll feel way more confident about what I’m doing at work. Like a purchase order will seem obvious to me, and I’ll feel like, okay, I’ve got this, I know where things are going, I can handle way more than people think I can. But then if someone questions it, or tells me to slow down, or acts like I need to explain myself more, I can get really irritated. Not just a little annoyed — I mean I’ll snap harder than I mean to. I’ll hear myself getting sharp, and part of me knows I’m coming off too intense, but in the moment it feels like they’re in my way or they’re not keeping up.\n\nSo… yeah, I do get those stretches where I’m more energized and confident, and sometimes it flips into being really irritable if people push back. The sleep part, I don’t know how to explain without overthinking it, but the main thing is that feeling like I can take on a lot more than usual, and then reacting badly when someone tries to slow me down.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat, energized, or confident usually last? Do they go away on their own, or do they last for days or even weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: It’s not like it’s just one afternoon and then I’m fine. That’s part of why it’s been hard to explain, because it kind of comes in stretches, and then I look back and realize I’ve been running hotter than normal for a while.\n\nI’d say these elevated, irritable, high-energy periods have been happening for about… maybe eleven months now, as part of my life. Not every single second of every day, but they’ve been coming up over that whole time. When I’m in it, it doesn’t feel brief. It can last long enough that people notice I’m acting different, and I don’t always come down from it just because I decide I should.\n\nSometimes it feels like it eases on its own eventually, but not in a clean way where I can say, “Oh, that was two hours and now it’s over.” It’s more like days where I’m more keyed up, more confident, more easily irritated, and then maybe it fades, and then later it comes back again. So overall, this has been going on for around eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually care about, where it was hard to function normally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly, but I do have these off days where I just kind of shut down. Like I’ll be in my apartment, and I’m not really doing anything, but somehow the whole day goes by. I’ll just sit there scrolling through sneaker drops, release calendars, resale pages, stuff like that — but not even in a way where I’m excited about it. It’s more like my thumb keeps moving and I’m staring at it, but I don’t really care if I buy anything or if something sells out or whatever.\n\nAnd people will text me, like friends asking if I want to play volleyball or go to a gallery night or just meet up, and I’ll see the message come in and feel this immediate… heaviness, I guess. Like answering would mean I have to become a person again and explain myself or make a plan, and it just feels like too much work. So I dodge the texts. I’ll tell myself I’ll answer in ten minutes, and then it’s hours later, or the next day, and then I feel worse because now it seems awkward to respond.\n\nIt’s not always that I’m crying or lying in bed dramatically or anything. It’s more like everything takes too much effort, even stuff I normally like or people I actually care about. I just sit there and let the day disappear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, or lost interest in most things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. The “two weeks” part is hard for me to pin down because I don’t really track it cleanly. I can definitely have days where I feel really flat and I don’t want to answer anybody or do anything, but whether it’s most of the day, nearly every day, for two full weeks… I don’t know if I can say that for sure.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll notice afterward that I’ve been kind of checked out for a while, but I don’t have a clear start and end date in my head. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just guessing. I know I get those stretches where everything feels like too much effort and things I usually like don’t really pull me in, but I’m not sure it’s been a solid two weeks in the way you’re asking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

I’ll ask to look at the reports again, or I’ll point out where the launch numbers don’t make sense to me, because in my head it feels like there’s something there that should show it.\n\nI haven’t been able to just let it go. But there are definitely times where I feel almost like everything is opening up for me, like the whole city is just… available, if that makes sense. So… no, I can’t say for sure that I’ve had that exact kind of two-week period.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you think of a time when someone you trusted, like your supervisor, showed you clear evidence that your concerns about the buys and launch numbers weren’t accurate — and if so, were you able to accept that evidence, or did you still feel certain that something was being hidden or manipulated in a way that wasn’t shown in the reports?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / wholesale_or_retail_buyer / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"What really made me come in is that I can't shake the feeling that people at work and even some vendors are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid, even when my supervisor shows me the reports don't prove that.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… that’s kind of the main thing that made me come in, honestly. Because there have been times where my supervisor will sit down with me and pull up the reports, like the buy quantities, launch numbers, sell-through, vendor confirmations, all of it, and he’ll be like, “Look, this doesn’t show what you’re saying. The numbers line up.” And I can see what he’s showing me. I’m not saying I can’t read a spreadsheet or anything.

But it doesn’t settle it for me. That’s the part that bothers me. I’ll look at the report and for a second I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m overthinking this… but then it’s like my brain immediately goes, yeah, but that’s just the report they’re showing you. Or the numbers were cleaned up before they got to me. Or the vendor gave one set of numbers to someone else and another set to me so I’d make the wrong call and look stupid.

It sounds bad when I say it out loud, but at work it feels really clear. Like if a launch underperforms after I pushed for certain units, I start thinking people knew it was going to happen and let me walk into it. Or if the numbers look too perfect, that also feels suspicious, like they’re trying too hard to make it look normal. So even when my supervisor is trying to be reasonable and show me evidence, I still feel like there’s something behind it that isn’t in the reports.

I don’t know how to explain it without sounding paranoid, but it feels like people at work — and sometimes vendors too — are watching what I buy, tracking my decisions, and messing with the launch numbers in a way that makes me look incompetent. And I know my supervisor gets frustrated because he’s like, “What evidence would convince you?” and I don’t really have a good answer, because the evidence itself feels like it could be part of what’s being manipulated.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess what finally made me come in is... it’s this thing at work that I can’t get out of my head. I work with buys and launch numbers, and I keep feeling like people at work — and honestly even some of the vendors — are tracking what I’m buying or changing things around, like messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid or like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nAnd I know how that sounds. My supervisor has shown me reports and said, basically, “Look, the numbers don’t prove that,” or that it’s just normal changes, but it still feels like there’s something going on behind it. I keep going over it and over it, and I can’t really shake it, and it’s starting to mess with me enough that I figured I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people or vendors at work are intentionally trying to harm or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word, like I’m not saying they’re trying to physically hurt me or anything. But deceive me? Make me look bad? Yeah, that’s what it feels like.\n\nIt’s like they’re watching what I’m buying, tracking the buys somehow, and then the launch numbers come out weird or get changed in a way that makes it look like I made a bad call. I’ll make a buying decision, or I’ll be watching how something is supposed to launch, and then the numbers come out in a way that feels off, like somebody knew what I was doing and adjusted things so I’d look wrong. And even when my supervisor pulls up the reports and says, “See, this doesn’t show anyone changed anything,” I still keep thinking maybe the report just doesn’t show the part that matters, or maybe they’re covering it in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo yeah, I’d say it’s mainly in the work environment. It’s not like every single person in my life feels suspicious to me. It’s more that at work, with the vendors and the tracking of buys, I can’t get rid of the feeling that something is being done on purpose to make me look stupid.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences outside of work where you felt people were acting together in secret or manipulating things in a way that was specifically targeted at you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. I mean, I can get in my head about it after I leave work, like I’ll be at home thinking through the launch numbers or replaying what a vendor said, but the actual feeling is still about work.\n\nIt’s not like I think my friends or random people are all secretly coordinating against me. It’s more specific than that. It’s the buys, the vendors, the reports, the launch numbers — that whole setup. That’s where it feels like people could be tracking what I’m buying or moving things around so it reflects badly on me.\n\nAnd that’s kind of why it bothers me so much, because if it was just some random thought everywhere, maybe I could dismiss it easier. But it feels tied to real stuff I’m responsible for. Even when my supervisor shows me reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I keep thinking, yeah, but what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they’re making it look normal on purpose?\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it really happens outside of work in the same way. It’s mainly there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever question whether your concerns about the buys and launch numbers might not be accurate, or do you feel certain that something is being done on purpose to make you look bad?\n\nPatient: I do question it sometimes, yeah. Like… that’s part of why I’m here. If I was completely sure, maybe I wouldn’t even be asking anyone about it. But it’s not like I can just talk myself out of it either.\n\nMy supervisor will show me the reports and say, basically, “Look, this doesn’t show anyone changed the launch numbers,” or “There’s no evidence the vendors are tracking your buys,” and in that moment I’ll try to listen. I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m reading too much into it. Maybe it’s just the numbers being weird, or normal changes, or whatever.\n\nBut then I leave and I keep going over it, and it starts feeling too specific again. Like the timing will line up with something I bought, or a launch will come out looking bad in a way that makes it seem like I made a stupid decision, and I can’t shake the feeling that someone is doing it on purpose. Not necessarily trying to hurt me physically or anything, but trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I’m not one hundred percent certain every second, but the feeling is really strong. Even when someone shows me proof that it doesn’t add up, I still think, well, what if the report just doesn’t show the part that matters? What if they know how to make it look normal? That’s what keeps getting stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any actions based on these suspicions — like confronting someone, gathering evidence, or reporting it to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly I’ve kept bringing it up at work, especially with my supervisor. I’ll ask to look at the reports again, or I’ll point out where the launch numbers don’t make sense to me, because in my head it feels like there’s something there that should show it.\n\nI haven’t been able to just let it go. Like, if a buy I made looks bad after the launch, I start going back through it and thinking, okay, who knew about this, who had access, did the vendor know what I was buying, did somebody adjust something after the fact? And then I’ll want the reports pulled up because I feel like there has to be some proof somewhere.\n\nBut the hard part is, even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anyone changed anything, I still don’t feel settled. I’ll sit there and think, “Okay, maybe,” but then another part of me is like, what if the report just isn’t showing the part that matters? Or what if they know how to make it look normal?\n\nSo I guess yes, I’ve acted on it in the sense that I’ve questioned it, checked things, and brought it to my supervisor. And honestly, what really made me come in is that I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when I’m shown that the reports don’t really prove that, it still keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs or experiences — even outside of work — where you felt certain something was happening that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Not really anything separate from that, no. I mean, I don’t have some whole other thing where I think strangers are doing something, or my friends are in on something, or anything like that. It mostly comes back to work, over and over.\n\nThe thing I can’t shake is the buys and the launch numbers. Like, I’ll make a buy, and then if the launch numbers come back in a way that makes the decision look dumb or like I missed something obvious, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like someone knew what I was doing, or was watching what I bought, and then something got moved around or adjusted so it would make me look bad.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds like I’m stuck on it, because I am. My supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me, like, “This doesn’t prove anyone changed anything,” or “There’s nothing here showing vendors are tracking your buys.” And I can sit there and understand what they’re saying, but I still leave with this feeling like the report just isn’t showing the part that matters. Like maybe they know how to make it look normal.\n\nSo if you mean other unusual beliefs outside of work, I don’t think so. But this one feels really real to me, even though other people don’t seem to see it the same way. That’s honestly the main reason I came in, because I keep trying to drop it and I just can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually upbeat or energized, maybe even more confident or irritable than usual, where you didn’t need much sleep or felt like you could take on a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to know what counts as “unusual” because sometimes I’ll tell myself I’m just having a good stretch, like I’m finally getting ahead of things. But there are definitely times where I feel almost like everything is opening up for me, like the whole city is just… available, if that makes sense. Like I can move faster, make decisions faster, see opportunities other people aren’t seeing.\n\nAnd when I’m in that kind of state, I’ll feel way more confident about what I’m doing at work. Like a purchase order will seem obvious to me, and I’ll feel like, okay, I’ve got this, I know where things are going, I can handle way more than people think I can. But then if someone questions it, or tells me to slow down, or acts like I need to explain myself more, I can get really irritated. Not just a little annoyed — I mean I’ll snap harder than I mean to. I’ll hear myself getting sharp, and part of me knows I’m coming off too intense, but in the moment it feels like they’re in my way or they’re not keeping up.\n\nSo… yeah, I do get those stretches where I’m more energized and confident, and sometimes it flips into being really irritable if people push back. The sleep part, I don’t know how to explain without overthinking it, but the main thing is that feeling like I can take on a lot more than usual, and then reacting badly when someone tries to slow me down.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat, energized, or confident usually last? Do they go away on their own, or do they last for days or even weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: It’s not like it’s just one afternoon and then I’m fine. That’s part of why it’s been hard to explain, because it kind of comes in stretches, and then I look back and realize I’ve been running hotter than normal for a while.\n\nI’d say these elevated, irritable, high-energy periods have been happening for about… maybe eleven months now, as part of my life. Not every single second of every day, but they’ve been coming up over that whole time. When I’m in it, it doesn’t feel brief. It can last long enough that people notice I’m acting different, and I don’t always come down from it just because I decide I should.\n\nSometimes it feels like it eases on its own eventually, but not in a clean way where I can say, “Oh, that was two hours and now it’s over.” It’s more like days where I’m more keyed up, more confident, more easily irritated, and then maybe it fades, and then later it comes back again. So overall, this has been going on for around eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually care about, where it was hard to function normally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly, but I do have these off days where I just kind of shut down. Like I’ll be in my apartment, and I’m not really doing anything, but somehow the whole day goes by. I’ll just sit there scrolling through sneaker drops, release calendars, resale pages, stuff like that — but not even in a way where I’m excited about it. It’s more like my thumb keeps moving and I’m staring at it, but I don’t really care if I buy anything or if something sells out or whatever.\n\nAnd people will text me, like friends asking if I want to play volleyball or go to a gallery night or just meet up, and I’ll see the message come in and feel this immediate… heaviness, I guess. Like answering would mean I have to become a person again and explain myself or make a plan, and it just feels like too much work. So I dodge the texts. I’ll tell myself I’ll answer in ten minutes, and then it’s hours later, or the next day, and then I feel worse because now it seems awkward to respond.\n\nIt’s not always that I’m crying or lying in bed dramatically or anything. It’s more like everything takes too much effort, even stuff I normally like or people I actually care about. I just sit there and let the day disappear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, or lost interest in most things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. The “two weeks” part is hard for me to pin down because I don’t really track it cleanly. I can definitely have days where I feel really flat and I don’t want to answer anybody or do anything, but whether it’s most of the day, nearly every day, for two full weeks… I don’t know if I can say that for sure.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll notice afterward that I’ve been kind of checked out for a while, but I don’t have a clear start and end date in my head. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just guessing. I know I get those stretches where everything feels like too much effort and things I usually like don’t really pull me in, but I’m not sure it’s been a solid two weeks in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you say for sure that you’ve had a period where you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that for sure, no. Like, I can say I’ve had stretches where I’m really low or just kind of shut off, and it feels like everything is heavy and pointless in the moment, but when you say “most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks straight,” I’m not confident enough to say yes.\n\nPart of the problem is I don’t mark it down or anything, so after the fact it all blurs together. I might have a few days where I barely respond to people and don’t really care about anything, then maybe one day where I’m sort of okay or distracted, and then I drop back into it. And honestly, what really made me come in is that I can’t shake the feeling that people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Even when I’m shown that the reports don’t really prove that, it still keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs or experiences — even outside of work — where you felt certain something was happening that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Not really anything separate from that, no. I mean, I don’t have some whole other thing where I think strangers are doing something, or my friends are in on something, or anything like that. It mostly comes back to work, over and over.\n\nThe thing I can’t shake is the buys and the launch numbers. Like, I’ll make a buy, and then if the launch numbers come back in a way that makes the decision look dumb or like I missed something obvious, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like someone knew what I was doing, or was watching what I bought, and then something got moved around or adjusted so it would make me look bad.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds like I’m stuck on it, because I am. My supervisor has pulled up reports and tried to show me, like, “This doesn’t prove anyone changed anything,” or “There’s nothing here showing vendors are tracking your buys.” And I can sit there and understand what they’re saying, but I still leave with this feeling like the report just isn’t showing the part that matters. Like maybe they know how to make it look normal.\n\nSo if you mean other unusual beliefs outside of work, I don’t think so. But this one feels really real to me, even though other people don’t seem to see it the same way. That’s honestly the main reason I came in, because I keep trying to drop it and I just can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually upbeat or energized, maybe even more confident or irritable than usual, where you didn’t need much sleep or felt like you could take on a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to know what counts as “unusual” because sometimes I’ll tell myself I’m just having a good stretch, like I’m finally getting ahead of things. But there are definitely times where I feel almost like everything is opening up for me, like the whole city is just… available, if that makes sense. Like I can move faster, make decisions faster, see opportunities other people aren’t seeing.\n\nAnd when I’m in that kind of state, I’ll feel way more confident about what I’m doing at work. Like a purchase order will seem obvious to me, and I’ll feel like, okay, I’ve got this, I know where things are going, I can handle way more than people think I can. But then if someone questions it, or tells me to slow down, or acts like I need to explain myself more, I can get really irritated. Not just a little annoyed — I mean I’ll snap harder than I mean to. I’ll hear myself getting sharp, and part of me knows I’m coming off too intense, but in the moment it feels like they’re in my way or they’re not keeping up.\n\nSo… yeah, I do get those stretches where I’m more energized and confident, and sometimes it flips into being really irritable if people push back. The sleep part, I don’t know how to explain without overthinking it, but the main thing is that feeling like I can take on a lot more than usual, and then reacting badly when someone tries to slow me down.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat, energized, or confident usually last? Do they go away on their own, or do they last for days or even weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: It’s not like it’s just one afternoon and then I’m fine. That’s part of why it’s been hard to explain, because it kind of comes in stretches, and then I look back and realize I’ve been running hotter than normal for a while.\n\nI’d say these elevated, irritable, high-energy periods have been happening for about… maybe eleven months now, as part of my life. Not every single second of every day, but they’ve been coming up over that whole time. When I’m in it, it doesn’t feel brief. It can last long enough that people notice I’m acting different, and I don’t always come down from it just because I decide I should.\n\nSometimes it feels like it eases on its own eventually, but not in a clean way where I can say, “Oh, that was two hours and now it’s over.” It’s more like days where I’m more keyed up, more confident, more easily irritated, and then maybe it fades, and then later it comes back again. So overall, this has been going on for around eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually care about, where it was hard to function normally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly, but I do have these off days where I just kind of shut down. Like I’ll be in my apartment, and I’m not really doing anything, but somehow the whole day goes by. I’ll just sit there scrolling through sneaker drops, release calendars, resale pages, stuff like that — but not even in a way where I’m excited about it. It’s more like my thumb keeps moving and I’m staring at it, but I don’t really care if I buy anything or if something sells out or whatever.\n\nAnd people will text me, like friends asking if I want to play volleyball or go to a gallery night or just meet up, and I’ll see the message come in and feel this immediate… heaviness, I guess. Like answering would mean I have to become a person again and explain myself or make a plan, and it just feels like too much work. So I dodge the texts. I’ll tell myself I’ll answer in ten minutes, and then it’s hours later, or the next day, and then I feel worse because now it seems awkward to respond.\n\nIt’s not always that I’m crying or lying in bed dramatically or anything. It’s more like everything takes too much effort, even stuff I normally like or people I actually care about. I just sit there and let the day disappear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, or lost interest in most things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. The “two weeks” part is hard for me to pin down because I don’t really track it cleanly. I can definitely have days where I feel really flat and I don’t want to answer anybody or do anything, but whether it’s most of the day, nearly every day, for two full weeks… I don’t know if I can say that for sure.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll notice afterward that I’ve been kind of checked out for a while, but I don’t have a clear start and end date in my head. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just guessing. Like, I can say I’ve had stretches where I’m really low or just kind of shut off, and it feels like everything is heavy and pointless in the moment, but when you say “most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks straight,” I’m not confident enough to say yes.\n\nPart of the problem is I don’t mark it down or anything, so after the fact it all blurs together.
